Method: 选取四个LLM调解者（三个开放权重模型、一个商业模型），使用DeliberationBank与Polis的意见数据。每个任务由调解者基于30条意见起草提案5次，分别改变随机种子或意见顺序。统计"五轮集合中出现部分轮次含分配、部分轮次不含分配"的比例；比较"重排意见"与"替换为不同公民意见"对分配的影响幅度；由三名人工标注者核验轮间差异的真实性，并与LLM评判结果对比；在四种干预（温度置零、商业调解者、草稿筛选、草稿合并）下检验变异是否残留；对比"寻求共识"与"描述分歧"两类提示对少数立场的覆盖度。

Result: 开放权重调解者17%、商业调解者20%的五轮集合中存在分配有无不一致。重排意见顺序造成的分配变化几乎与替换为不同公民意见相当。三名人工标注者确认轮间差异真实存在，但LLM评判高估了其发生频率。在温度置零、商业调解者、草稿筛选、草稿合并四种防护下仍存在显著变异。提示目标影响代表性："寻求共识"提示对少数立场的覆盖显著低于"描述分歧"提示。

Conclusion: 单一提案会同时遮蔽其提出的分配选择与被排除的观点，输出变异是结构性而非可调参数问题。作者建议开展两类审计：一是使替代分配方案可见（多轮采样并披露分布），二是披露每个提案背后的目标设定（共识导向或分歧描述）。

Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly being used to turn citizens' opinions into collective proposals. These proposals can make assignments: who should act, what must be done, how much one should spend and by when. However, if the assignments change when the same opinions are processed again, publishing a single proposal can hide alternatives that participants never get to see. We examined this issue in four LLM mediators (three open-weight and one commercial) using opinions from DeliberationBank and Polis. For each task, a mediator drafted a proposal from thirty opinions five times, varying either the random seed or the order of the opinions. In 17% of five-run sets from the open-weight mediators and 20% from the commercial one, some runs contained assignments while others did not. Reordering the opinions changed the assignments almost as often as replacing them with opinions from different citizens. Three human annotators confirmed that the detected differences between runs were real, although the LLM judge overstated how often they occurred. Substantial variation remained under four safeguards: temperature zero, a commercial mediator, draft selection, and draft merging. The mediator's objective also shaped representation: prompts seeking agreement covered minority positions much less often than prompts asking to describe differences. A single proposal can therefore obscure choices in both the assignments it proposes and the views it excludes. We propose audits that make alternative assignments visible and disclose the objective behind each proposal.

</details>


### [310] [Meta$^2$: Precomputing Reusable Knowledge for Online Harness Evolution in Long-Horizon Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05139)
*Lingzhi Yuan,Yiguang Cai,Weize Liu,Siqi Yang,Feijiang Han,Yongkyun Lee,Udari Madhushani Sehwag,Furong Huang*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Meta$^2$ 将离线优化从"优化共享 harness"上移一层，改为学习可复用的文本化 Meta-Skill $M$，在任务在线阶段指导 harness 演化决策（KEEP/EVOLVE），实现跨任务知识迁移与任务局部编辑。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有 harness 优化存在两难：离线方法可学习已完成轨迹，但部署单一共享 harness 易过拟合训练任务、且无法在运行中响应新证据；在线演化虽可即时适应，但需从零推理每次干预，或依赖测试时通常不可得的验证器反馈。

Method: 离线阶段通过对比同一任务 agent 在三种配置下的 rollout（无 evolver、由当前 $M$ 引导的 evolver、以及仅训练期可用的特权验证器信息引导的 evolver）来学习文本化 Meta-Skill $M$，跨任务聚合轨迹与结果差异，提炼"何时/如何演化、何时保持不变、应避免哪些干预"的可复用指导。在线阶段 evolver 将冻结的 $M$ 与涌现的轨迹证据结合，决定 KEEP 还是通过任务局部编辑 EVOLVE，编辑作用于同一次运行的后续部分且不重置环境或进度。

Result: 在 Terminal-Bench 2.1 与 DeepSWE 1.1 两个长时程基准上，使用 GPT-5.6-Terra 时分别达到 81.00% 与 44.44%，为所评估方法中最高；消融验证了对比式演化指导与在线编辑保持任务局部的价值。

Conclusion: 将离线优化提升至"学习如何演化 harness"的元层面，可在不共享具体 harness 修改的前提下实现跨任务迁移，同时保持在线适应性与测试时无需验证器反馈的可行性。

Abstract: Harness optimization improves long-horizon agents by refining the prompts, tools, memory, and hooks around a frozen model. Offline methods can learn from completed trajectories, but deploy one shared harness that may overfit training tasks and cannot react to evidence revealed during a new run. Online evolution can adapt before, during, or after one run through an evolver, but may have to reason about every intervention from scratch or rely on verifier feedback that is usually unavailable at test time. We introduce Meta$^2$, which moves offline optimization one level up: rather than optimizing a shared harness, it learns reusable knowledge for harness evolution and applies it within each task online. Offline, Meta$^2$ learns a textual Meta-Skill $M$ by contrasting training rollouts of the same task agent under three configurations: without an evolver, with an evolver guided by the current version of $M$, and with an evolver given privileged verifier information available only in training. Contrasts in trajectories and outcomes are aggregated across tasks to refine $M$ into reusable guidance on when and how to evolve, when to leave the harness unchanged, and which interventions to avoid. Online, an evolver combines the frozen $M$ with emerging trajectory evidence to decide whether to KEEP the harness unchanged or EVOLVE it through a task-local edit. Each edit applies to the rest of the same run without resetting its environment or progress. Thus, evolution guidance transfers across tasks while harness edits remain task-local, and held-out verifier feedback is never available to the evolver. On two long-horizon benchmarks, Terminal-Bench 2.1 and DeepSWE 1.1, Meta$^2$ achieves the highest performance among evaluated methods with GPT-5.6-Terra, reaching 81.00% and 44.44%, respectively. Ablations support the value of contrastive evolution guidance and keeping online harness edits task-local.

</details>


### [311] [WMIP: A Working-Memory-Inspired Programmable Probe of Cumulative State Tracking in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.27343)
*Dengzhe Hou,Lingyu Jiang,Deng Li,Zirui Li,Fangzhou Lin,Kazunori D Yamada*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: 论文提出一种受工作记忆启发的"累积状态跟踪"探针任务（3–100次更新、答案由程序计算、含最终/中间/多目标查询），在18个LLM上比较不同输出协议，发现单步算术准确率84.9%但核心项仅31.7%，50次更新以上无模型超过10%，且输出协议（写步骤+末行作答）比预算更能提升准确率。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LLM需在不写出中间状态的前提下跟踪单条提示内的多次更新，类似人类工作记忆；但现有测试对"是否允许写中间步骤"的约束不一致，导致结果不可比，缺乏对累积状态跟踪能力的受控评测。

Method: 设计工作记忆启发的探针：程序生成答案、3–100次更新、三类查询（最终状态/中间状态/多目标），在完全相同的题目上对比多种输出协议；评测18个LLM，并与公共Berkeley Function Calling Leaderboard分数做相关性分析。

Result: 单步算术84.9%，需3–7次更新后给出单一数值的核心项仅31.7%；50次更新以上无模型超过10%；更大token预算配合"写步骤并把答案放最后一行"的指令使核心准确率升至88.0%，提升主要来自指令而非预算；核心准确率与BFCL分数正相关。

Conclusion: 强单步算术能力不能保证可靠的累积状态跟踪；输出协议显著改变测得准确率，因此跨研究的比较必须控制协议；作者开源生成器、题目、评分代码与模型回答以支持可复现对比。

Abstract: Human working memory lets people maintain and update information. Large language models (LLMs) face a related demand when tracking updates within a single prompt without writing out intermediate states, yet existing tests differ in their constraints on written steps. We introduce a working-memory-inspired probe of cumulative state tracking with program-computed answers, 3 to 100 updates, and final-state, intermediate-state, or multi-target queries. It compares protocols on identical items. Across 18 LLMs, accuracy is 84.9% on single-step arithmetic but 31.7% on core items requiring one number after three to seven updates. From 50 updates on, no model exceeds 10% accuracy. Core accuracy reaches 88.0% under a larger budget and instructions to write steps and place the answer on the last line, mainly due to the instruction changes rather than the budget. Core accuracy also correlates positively with public Berkeley Function Calling Leaderboard scores. These findings show that strong single-step arithmetic does not ensure reliable cumulative tracking and that output protocols substantially change measured accuracy. We release the generator, items, scoring code, and responses for reproducible comparisons across depths, query types, and protocols.

</details>


### [312] [Computational Phenomenology of Borderline Personality Disorder: A Comparative Evaluation of LLM-Simulated Expert Personas and Human Clinical Experts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.19008)
*Marcin Moskalewicz,Anna Sterna,Karolina Drożdż,Kacper Dudzic,Marek Pokropski,Paula Flores*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: 该研究以人类主导的主题分析为基线（边缘型人格障碍住院患者临床生活史访谈，>15万词），用混合方法评估GPT、Gemini、Claude辅助质性临床分析的能力。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 探讨大语言模型能否支撑质性临床分析，并检验其在缓解人类研究者偏差、逼近人类解释质量方面的潜力。

Method: 三项子研究：Study A由现象学与临床心理学专家在盲/非盲条件下，用语义一致性、Jaccard系数及多维效度评分（可信度、连贯性、结果实质性、数据扎根性）评判AI生成内容；Study B用神经嵌入方法将人与模型的主题描述映射到多维向量空间，客观量化语义与语言风格差异；Study C开展非专家评估（N=115），考察主题冗长程度对“人类作者身份”感知与内容效度的影响。

Result: AI分析与人类解释的重叠度高度可变（0–58%）；所有模型均识别出人类研究者遗漏的主题，显示其缓解人类偏差的能力；在主题层面，外部评估者无法可靠区分人类与AI撰写的主题；盲评条件下，Gemini 2.5 Pro的内容效度表现与人类不可区分，且其语义向量嵌入风格最接近人类。

Conclusion: 大语言模型可部分复现并补充人类质性临床分析，具备缓解研究者偏差的潜力；其中Gemini 2.5 Pro在效度与风格相似性上最接近人类，但整体重叠度差异大，说明模型输出尚不能替代人类解释。

Abstract: Building on a human-led thematic analysis of clinical life-story interviews (> 150,000 words) with inpatients with Borderline Personality Disorder, this study examines the capacity of large language models (OpenAI's GPT, Google's Gemini, and Anthropic's Claude) to support qualitative clinical analysis. The models' interpretative potential was evaluated using a mixed-methods approach. Study A involved blinded and non-blinded judges in phenomenology and clinical psychology. The experts assessed the validity of AI-generated content using semantic congruence, Jaccard coefficients, and multidimensional validity ratings, including credibility, coherence, the substantiveness of results, and grounding in qualitative data. In Study B, neural methods were used to embed human- and model-generated theme descriptions in a multidimensional vector space. This approach provided an objectified computational measure of the difference between human and model semantics and linguistic style. In Study C, complementary non-expert evaluations were conducted (N=115) to examine the influence of thematic verbosity on the perception of human authorship and content validity. Overall, the results of AI analysis showed a highly variable overlap (0-58%) with the human interpretation, while all models identified themes originally omitted by human researchers, proving their capacity to mitigate human bias. At the thematic level, external evaluators were unable to reliably distinguish human-authored themes from those generated by AI. In terms of content validity assessed against raw data by high-level experts in the blinded mode, the performance of Gemini 2.5 Pro was indistinguishable from that of humans. The comparison of semantic vector embeddings showed that its style was also the closest to humans.

</details>
